For $I_{2}$ , using the expressions of $M(U,D)$ and $S_{U}(D)$ , we obtain that (4.58) $\displaystyle I_{2}=$ $\displaystyle-4\epsilon(\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla\Lambda^{s}\eta\,|\,\Lambda^{s}\eta)_{2}-2\{((1+\epsilon\eta-\epsilon\Delta)\nabla\cdot\Lambda^{s}\boldsymbol{u}\,|\,\Lambda^{s}\eta)_{2}$ $\displaystyle+(\nabla\Lambda^{s}\eta\,|\,(1+\epsilon\eta-\epsilon\Delta)\Lambda^{s}\boldsymbol{u})_{2}\}$ $\displaystyle-2\epsilon^{2}\{(\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla\Lambda^{s}\eta\,|\,\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\Lambda^{s}\boldsymbol{u})_{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}(\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\partial_{i}\Lambda^{s}\boldsymbol{u}\,|\,u_{i}\Lambda^{s}\eta)_{2}\}$ $\displaystyle-2\epsilon\{((1+\epsilon\eta-\epsilon\Delta)\nabla\cdot\Lambda^{s}\boldsymbol{u}\,|\,\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\Lambda^{s}\boldsymbol{u})_{2}$ $\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}(\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\partial_{i}\Lambda^{s}\boldsymbol{u}\,|\,(1+\epsilon\eta-\epsilon\Delta)\Lambda^{s}u_{i})_{2}\}$ $\displaystyle\underset{\text{def}}{=}I_{21}+I_{22}+I_{23}+I_{24}.$ Integrating by parts, we get that $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &I_{21}=2\epsilon(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{u}\Lambda^{s}\eta\,|\,\Lambda^{s}\eta)_{2},\quad I_{22}=2\epsilon(\nabla\eta\cdot\Lambda^{s}\boldsymbol{u}\,|\,\Lambda^{s}\eta)_{2},\\
&I_{23}=2\epsilon^{2}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{u}\Lambda^{s}\eta\,|\,\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\Lambda^{s}\boldsymbol{u})_{2}+2\epsilon^{2}(\Lambda^{s}\boldsymbol{u}\,|\,(\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla)\boldsymbol{u}\Lambda^{s}\eta)_{2},\\
&I_{24}=2\epsilon^{2}(\nabla\eta\cdot\Lambda^{s}\boldsymbol{u}\,|\,\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\Lambda^{s}\boldsymbol{u})_{2}+2\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{2}((1+\epsilon\eta)\Lambda^{s}u_{i}\,|\,\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\Lambda^{s}\boldsymbol{u})_{2}\\
&\qquad\quad+2\epsilon^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}(\nabla\Lambda^{s}u_{i}\,|\,\nabla(\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\Lambda^{s}\boldsymbol{u}))_{2},\end{aligned}$ which along with ( 4.50 ) and ( 4.51 ) implies that (4.59) $|I_{2}|\lesssim\epsilon|{\boldsymbol{u}}|_{H^{s}}\bigl(|\eta|_{H^{s}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{u}|_{X^{s}_{\epsilon}}^{2}\bigr).$ Thanks to ( 4.57 ) and ( 4.59 ), we obtain that (4.60) $|I|\lesssim\epsilon|{\boldsymbol{u}}|_{H^{s}}\bigl(|\eta|_{H^{s}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{u}|_{X^{s}_{\epsilon}}^{2}\bigr).$ Due to ( 1.2 ) with $b=c=d=0,a=-1$ , we deduce by using ( 4.50 ) and ( 4.51 ) that (4.61) $|\partial_{t}\eta|_{H^{s-2}}+|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}|_{H^{s-1}}\lesssim|\eta|_{H^{s}}+|\boldsymbol{u}|_{X^{s}_{\epsilon}}.$ Combining ( 4.53 ), ( 4.54 ), ( 4.60 ) and ( 4.61 ), we finally get that (4.62) $\frac{d}{dt}E_{s}(U)\lesssim\epsilon\bigl(|\eta|_{H^{s}}+|\boldsymbol{u}|_{X^{s}_{\epsilon}}\bigr)\bigl(|\eta|_{H^{s}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{u}|_{X^{s}_{\epsilon}}^{2}\bigr).$ Due to ( 4.52 ), there holds (4.63) $\frac{d}{dt}\bigl(E_{s}(U)\bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C_{1}\epsilon E_{s}(U).$ Then following the same line as the proofs of Theorems 4.3 and 4.4 , one obtains that there exists $T>0$ independent of $\epsilon$ such that ( 1.2 )-( 4.1 ) has a unique solution on time interval $[0,T/\epsilon]$ . Moreover, ( 4.47 ) holds and Theorem 4.5 is proved.
∎ Remark 4.2 . The modelling of internal waves at the interface of a two-fluid system with different densities and in the presence of a rigid top leads, in an appropriate regime, to Boussinesq systems that are similar to those studied in the previous sections (see [ 8 ] , section 3.1.3) and for which one can obtain the same results as in [ 36 ] or in the present paper. The same regime for a two-fluid system but with a free upper surface has been considered in [ 16 ] , section 2.3.2. One gets a system of four equations for which the methods of [ 36 ] and of the present paper are likely to work, including the case of a slowing varying bottom. We also refer to [ 17 ] for further investigations on those extended Boussinesq systems, in particular for a construction of symmetrizable ones (modulo $\epsilon^{2}$ terms). 4.4. The difficult case $a=b=d=0,c<0$ . The method to solve the long time existence for this case is quite different from the other cases we dealt in the previous subsections and in the paper [ 36 ] . We will now quasilinearize the system by applying time together with space derivatives. The key point here is that we improve the regularity in space by improving the regularity in time (applying space derivatives to the system would cause a loss of derivatives). We first state the long time existence result in the one dimensional case : Theorem 4.6 . Let $a=b=d=0,c=-1$ .
Assume that $\eta_{0}\in X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}(\mathbb{R}),u_{0}\in X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}(\mathbb{R})$ satisfy the (non-cavitation) condition (4.64) $1+\epsilon\eta_{0}\geq H>0,\quad H\in(0,1),$ Then there exists a constant $\tilde{c}_{0}$ such that for any $\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{0}=\frac{1}{\tilde{c}_{0}(|\eta_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}+|u_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}})}$ , there
exists $T>0$ independent of $\epsilon$ , such that ( 3.21 )-( 4.1 ) has a unique solution $(\eta,u)$ with $\eta\in C([0,T/\epsilon];X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}(\mathbb{R}))$ and $u\in C([0,T/\epsilon];X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}(\mathbb{R}))$ . Moreover, (4.65) $\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T/\epsilon]}\bigl(|\eta|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}+|\eta_{t}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|\eta_{tt}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|u|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|u_{t}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|u_{tt}|_{2}^{2}\bigr)$ $\displaystyle\leq C\bigl(|\eta_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}+|u_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}\bigr).$ Remark 4.3 . System ( 3.21 ) can be viewed as the Saint-Venant (shallow water) system with surface tension and corresponds to system (A.1) in [ 18 ] with $\mu=0,\delta=1.$ Thus the previous theorem can be compared to Theorem A.3 in [ 18 ] . Remark 4.4 . It will be clear in the following proof that the regularity we choose for the initial data is the lowest possible one. One could also impose higher regularity on the initial data such as $\eta_{0}\in X^{2+k}_{\epsilon^{3+k}}(\mathbb{R}),u_{0}\in X^{2+k}_{\epsilon^{2+k}}(\mathbb{R})$ for $k\in\mathbb{N}$ . In that case, one has to apply $\partial_{t}$ to ( 1.2 ) for $k+2$ times. For simplicity, we only consider the case $k=0$ . Proof. We shall divide the proof into several steps. We first indicate how to obtain the a priori energy estimates. We shall use the a priori estimates to prove the existence of the solutions, in Section 5. Step 1. Reduction of the system. Since $a=b=d=0,c=-1$ , we rewrite ( 1.2 ) in the form ( 3.21 ). Setting $\displaystyle v\underset{\text{def}}{=}(1+\epsilon\eta)u,$ the first equation of ( 3.21 ) becomes $\displaystyle\eta_{t}+v_{x}=0.$ Elementary calculations and the use of ( 3.21 ) yield the evolution equation for $v:$ $\displaystyle v_{t}+(1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{x}-\epsilon(1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{xxx}+\epsilon\Bigl(\frac{v^{2}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{x}=0.$ Indeed, we have $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}v&=(1+\epsilon\eta)u_{t}+\epsilon u\eta_{t}\\
&=-(1+\epsilon\eta)(\eta_{x}-\epsilon\eta_{xxx})-\epsilon(1+\epsilon\eta)uu_{x}-\epsilon uv_{x}\\
&=-(1+\epsilon\eta)(\eta_{x}-\epsilon\eta_{xxx})-\epsilon\Bigl(\frac{v^{2}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{x}.\end{aligned}$ Then ( 3.21 ) is rewritten in terms of $(\eta,v)$ as follows (4.66) $\left\{\begin{aligned} &\eta_{t}+v_{x}=0,\\
&\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}v_{t}+\eta_{x}-\epsilon\eta_{xxx}+\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigl(\frac{v^{2}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{x}=0.\end{aligned}\right.$ We shall derive energy estimates for this system. Step 2. Quasilinearization of ( 4.66 ). In this step, we shall quasilinearize the system ( 4.66 )
by applying to it $\partial_{t}$ and $\partial_{t}^{2}$ . Applying $\partial_{t}$ to the first equation of ( 4.66 ) leads to $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}^{2}\eta&=-\partial_{x}v_{t}=\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{x}\bigr)_{x}-\epsilon\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{xxx}\bigr)_{x}+\epsilon\Bigl(\frac{v^{2}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{xx}\\
&=\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{x}\bigr)_{x}-\epsilon\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{xxx}\bigr)_{x}+\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}v_{xx}\\
&\qquad+2\epsilon\frac{v_{x}^{2}}{1+\epsilon\eta}-\frac{2\epsilon^{2}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}vv_{x}\eta_{x}-\epsilon^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\eta_{x}v^{2}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\Bigr)_{x}.\end{aligned}$ One notices that the last term $\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}v_{xx}$ in the second line of the above equality is the higher order term. Since by ( 4.66 ) $v_{x}=-\eta_{t}$ , we rewrite this term as $\displaystyle\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}v_{xx}=-\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\partial_{x}\eta_{t}.$ Then we obtain (4.67) $\eta_{tt}-\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{x}\bigr)_{x}+\epsilon\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{xxx}\bigr)_{x}+\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\partial_{x}\eta_{t}=f,$ with (4.68) $f\underset{\text{def}}{=}2\epsilon\frac{v_{x}^{2}}{1+\epsilon\eta}-\frac{2\epsilon^{2}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}vv_{x}\eta_{x}-\epsilon^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\eta_{x}v^{2}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\Bigr)_{x}.$ Applying $\partial_{t}$ to the second equation of ( 4.66 ) one obtains $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}^{2}v&=-(1+\epsilon\eta)\partial_{x}\eta_{t}+\epsilon(1+\epsilon\eta)\partial_{x}^{3}\eta_{t}-\epsilon\eta_{t}(\eta_{x}-\epsilon\eta_{xxx})-\epsilon\Bigl(\frac{v^{2}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{xt}\\
&=(1+\epsilon\eta)v_{xx}-\epsilon(1+\epsilon\eta)v_{xxxx}-\epsilon\eta_{t}(\eta_{x}-\epsilon\eta_{xxx})\\
&\qquad-2\epsilon v\partial_{x}\bigl(\frac{v_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)-\frac{2\epsilon v_{x}v_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}+\epsilon^{2}\Bigl(\frac{v^{2}\eta_{t}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\Bigr)_{x}.\end{aligned}$ Then we get (4.69) $\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}v_{tt}-v_{xx}+\epsilon v_{xxxx}+\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\partial_{x}\bigl(\frac{v_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)=g,$ with (4.70) $g\underset{\text{def}}{=}-\frac{\epsilon\eta_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}(\eta_{x}-\epsilon\eta_{xxx})-\frac{2\epsilon v_{x}v_{t}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigl(\frac{v^{2}\eta_{t}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\Bigr)_{x}.$ Combining ( 4.67 ) and ( 4.69 ), we obtain (4.71) $\left\{\begin{aligned} &\eta_{tt}-\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{x}\bigr)_{x}+\epsilon\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{xxx}\bigr)_{x}+\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\partial_{x}\eta_{t}=f,\\
&\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}v_{tt}-v_{xx}+\epsilon v_{xxxx}+\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\partial_{x}\bigl(\frac{v_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)=g,\end{aligned}\right.$ with $(f,g)$ being defined in ( 4.68 ) and ( 4.70 ). We remark here that ( 4.71 ) is a diagonalization of ( 4.66 ) and that the principal linear part for both equations of ( 4.71 ) is the dispersive wave equation $\displaystyle(\partial_{t}^{2}-\partial_{x}^{2}+\epsilon\partial_{x}^{4})\Psi.$ The source terms $(f,g)$ are of lower order. One can then derive the $L^{2}$ energy estimate for ( 4.71 ). However, if we want to derive higher order energy estimates, it is not successful to apply $\partial_{x}$ to the second equation of ( 4.71 ) since when $\partial_{x}$ acts on the term $\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}v_{tt}$ , it will turn out an uncontrolled term $-\frac{\epsilon\eta_{x}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}v_{tt}$ .
One has to apply instead $\partial_{t}$ to ( 4.71 ). In other words, we shall improve the regularity of the unknowns by applying $\partial_{t}^{k}$ (not $\partial_{x}^{\alpha}$ ) to ( 4.66 ). Denoting by $\eta^{\prime}=\partial_{t}\eta$ and $v^{\prime}=\partial_{t}v$ , applying $\partial_{t}$ to ( 4.71 ), it transpires that $(\eta^{\prime},v^{\prime})$ satisfies the following system (4.72) $\left\{\begin{aligned} &\eta^{\prime}_{tt}-\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta^{\prime}_{x}\bigr)_{x}+\epsilon\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta^{\prime}_{xxx}\bigr)_{x}+\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\partial_{x}\eta^{\prime}_{t}=f^{\prime},\\
&\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}v^{\prime}_{tt}-v^{\prime}_{xx}+\epsilon v^{\prime}_{xxxx}+\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\partial_{x}\bigl(\frac{v^{\prime}_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)=g^{\prime},\end{aligned}\right.$ where (4.73) $\displaystyle f^{\prime}\underset{\text{def}}{=}\partial_{t}f+\epsilon(\eta_{t}\eta_{x})_{x}-\epsilon^{2}(\eta_{t}\eta_{xxx})_{x}-2\epsilon\Bigl(\frac{v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{t}\eta_{tx},$ $\displaystyle g^{\prime}\underset{\text{def}}{=}\partial_{t}g+\frac{\epsilon\eta_{t}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}v_{tt}+\frac{2\epsilon^{2}v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigl(\frac{\eta_{t}v_{t}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\Bigr)_{x}-2\epsilon\Bigl(\frac{v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{t}\Bigl(\frac{v_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{x}.$ The principal part of ( 4.72 ) is the same as that of ( 4.71 ). Step 3. Energy estimates for the quasilinear system ( 4.66 )-( 4.71 )-( 4.72 ). We shall derive energy estimates for ( 4.66 ), ( 4.71 ) and ( 4.72 ) under the assumptions (4.74) $1+\epsilon\eta\geq H>0,$ and (4.75) $|\eta(\cdot,t)|_{W^{1,\infty}}+|v(\cdot,t)|_{W^{1,\infty}}+|\eta(\cdot,t)_{t}|_{\infty}+|v(\cdot,t)_{t}|_{\infty}\leq c,\quad\text{for}\;t\in[0,T],$ where the constant $c$ is independent of $\epsilon$ but depends on the initial data. We remark that ( 4.74 ) and ( 4.75 ) are consequences of the assumption ( 4.64 ) and the a priori estimate ( 4.107 ) for $(\eta,v)$ . Step 3.1. Estimates for ( 4.66 ). We notice that the symmetrizer for the linear part of ( 4.66 ) is the matrix $\text{diag}(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2},1)$ . Then taking the $L^{2}$ inner product of ( 4.66 ) by $\bigl((1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta,v\bigr)^{T}$ leads to (4.76) $\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{0}(t)=-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\bigl(\frac{\eta_{t}v}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\,|\,v\bigr)_{2}-\epsilon\bigl(\Bigl(\frac{v^{2}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{x}\,|\,\frac{v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)_{2},$ where $\displaystyle E_{0}(t)\underset{\text{def}}{=}|\eta|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\eta_{x}|_{2}^{2}+(\frac{v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\,|\,v)_{2}.$ Thanks to ( 4.74 ) and ( 4.75 ), we have (4.77) $E_{0}(t)\sim|\eta|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\eta_{x}|_{2}^{2}+|v|_{L^{2}}^{2}$ By ( 4.74 ), the first term on the r.h.s of ( 4.76 ) is estimated as $\displaystyle|-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\bigl(\frac{\eta_{t}v}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\,|\,v\bigr)_{2}|\lesssim\epsilon|v|_{\infty}|\eta_{t}|_{2}|v|_{2}\lesssim\epsilon|v|_{\infty}(|\eta_{t}|_{2}^{2}+|v|_{2}^{2}),$ while by integration by parts and ( 4.74 ), the second term on the r.h.s of ( 4.76 ) is estimated as $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &|-\epsilon\bigl(\Bigl(\frac{v^{2}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{x}\,|\,\frac{v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)_{2}|=\frac{\epsilon}{2}|\bigl(v_{x}\,|\,|\frac{v}{1+\epsilon\eta}|^{2}\bigr)_{2}|\\
&\lesssim\epsilon|v|_{\infty}|v|_{2}|v_{x}|_{2}\lesssim\epsilon|v|_{\infty}(|v|_{2}^{2}+|v_{x}|_{2}^{2}).\end{aligned}$ Then we obtain (4.78) $\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{0}(t)\lesssim\epsilon|v|_{\infty}\bigl(|\eta_{t}|_{2}^{2}+|v|_{2}^{2}+|v_{x}|_{2}^{2}\bigr).$ Step 3.2. Estimates for ( 4.71 ). Taking the $L^{2}$ scalar product of the first equation of ( 4.71 ) by $(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t}$ , we obtain $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &(\eta_{tt}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t})_{2}-(\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{x}\bigr)_{x}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t})_{2}\\
&\quad+\epsilon(\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{xxx}\bigr)_{x}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t})_{2}+(\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\partial_{x}\eta_{t}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t})_{2}=(f\,|\,(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t})_{2}.\end{aligned}$ Integration by parts gives $\displaystyle(\eta_{tt}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t})_{2}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\bigl(|\eta_{t}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\eta_{tx}|_{2}^{2}\bigr),$ $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &-(\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{x}\bigr)_{x}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t})_{2}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\bigl(((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{x}\,|\,\eta_{x})_{2}+\epsilon((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{xx}\,|\,\eta_{xx})_{2}\bigr)\\
&\qquad-\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\eta_{t}\eta_{x}\,|\,\eta_{x})_{2}-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}(\eta_{t}\eta_{xx}\,|\,\eta_{xx})_{2}-\epsilon^{2}(\partial_{x}(\eta_{x}\eta_{x})\,|\,\eta_{tx})_{2},\end{aligned}$ $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\epsilon(\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{xxx}\bigr)_{x}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t})_{2}=\frac{\epsilon}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\bigl(((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{xx}\,|\,\eta_{xx})_{2}+\epsilon((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{xxx}\,|\,\eta_{xxx})_{2}\bigr)\\
&\qquad-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}(\eta_{t}\eta_{xx}\,|\,\eta_{xx})_{2}-\frac{\epsilon^{3}}{2}(\eta_{t}\eta_{xxx}\,|\,\eta_{xxx})_{2}+\epsilon^{2}(\eta_{x}\eta_{xx}\,|\,\eta_{tx})_{2}.\end{aligned}$ Then we obtain that (4.79) $\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{11}(t)+(\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\partial_{x}\eta_{t}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t})_{2}$ $\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\eta_{t}\eta_{x}\,|\,\eta_{x})_{2}+\epsilon^{2}(\eta_{t}\eta_{xx}\,|\,\eta_{xx})_{2}+\epsilon^{2}(\eta_{xx}\eta_{x}\,|\,\eta_{tx})_{2}$ $\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon^{3}}{2}(\eta_{t}\eta_{xxx}\,|\,\eta_{xxx})_{2}+(f\,|\,(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t})_{2}$ where $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} E_{11}(t)&\underset{\text{def}}{=}|\eta_{t}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\eta_{tx}|_{2}^{2}+((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{x}\,|\,\eta_{x})_{2}+2\epsilon((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{xx}\,|\,\eta_{xx})_{2}\\
&\quad+\epsilon^{2}((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{xxx}\,|\,\eta_{xxx})_{2}.\end{aligned}$ By ( 4.74 ) and ( 4.75 ), we have (4.80) $E_{11}(t)\sim|\eta_{t}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\eta_{tx}|_{2}^{2}+|\eta_{x}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\eta_{xx}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\eta_{xxx}|_{2}^{2}.$ Now, we estimate the second term on the l.h.s of ( 4.79 ). Integrating by parts, we have $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &-(\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\partial_{x}\eta_{t}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t})_{2}=-\epsilon(\frac{v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\,|\,\partial_{x}(|\eta_{t}|^{2})-\epsilon\partial_{x}(|\eta_{tx}|^{2}))_{2}\\
&=\epsilon(\partial_{x}\Bigl(\frac{v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)\,|\,|\eta_{t}|^{2}-\epsilon|\eta_{tx}|^{2})_{2},\end{aligned}$ which along with ( 4.74 ) and ( 4.75 ) implies that (4.81) $|(\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\partial_{x}\eta_{t}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t})_{2}|\lesssim\epsilon\bigl(|\eta_{x}|_{\infty}+|v_{x}|_{\infty}\bigr)\bigl(|\eta_{t}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\eta_{tx}|_{2}^{2}\bigr).$ Due to ( 4.79 ) and ( 4.81 ), we get (4.82) $\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{11}(t)\lesssim\epsilon\bigl(|\eta_{t}|_{\infty}+|\eta_{x}|_{\infty}+|v_{x}|_{\infty}\bigr)\bigl(|\eta_{x}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\eta_{xx}|_{2}^{2}$ $\displaystyle+\epsilon^{2}|\eta_{xxx}|_{2}^{2}+|\eta_{t}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\eta_{tx}|_{2}^{2}\bigr)+|f|_{2}|\eta_{t}|_{2}+\epsilon|f_{x}|_{2}|\eta_{tx}|_{2}.$ Taking the $L^{2}$ scalar product of the second equation of ( 4.71 ) by $v_{t}$ yields (4.83) $\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{12}(t)+(\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\partial_{x}\Bigl(\frac{v_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)\,|\,v_{t})_{2}=-\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{t}\bigl(\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)v_{t}\,|\,v_{t})_{2}+(g\,|\,v_{t})_{2}$ where $\displaystyle E_{12}(t)\underset{\text{def}}{=}(\frac{v_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\,|\,v_{t})_{2}+|v_{x}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|v_{xx}|_{2}^{2}.$ Thanks to ( 4.74 ), we have (4.84) $E_{12}(t)\sim|v_{t}|_{2}^{2}+|v_{x}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|v_{xx}|_{2}^{2}.$ Similarly as for ( 4.81 ), integration by parts on the second term on the l.h.s of ( 4.83 ) leads to (4.85) $|(\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\partial_{x}\Bigl(\frac{v_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)\,|\,v_{t})_{2}|=\epsilon|(\partial_{x}v\,|\,\bigl|\frac{v_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr|^{2})_{2}|\lesssim\epsilon|v_{x}|_{\infty}|v_{t}|_{2}^{2}.$ We can also bound the first term on the r.h.s of ( 4.83 ) as follows $\displaystyle|-\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{t}\bigl(\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)v_{t}\,|\,v_{t})_{2}|\lesssim\epsilon|\eta_{t}|_{\infty}|v_{t}|_{2}^{2},$ which along with ( 4.83 ) and ( 4.85 ) gives rise to (4.86) $\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{12}(t)\lesssim\epsilon\bigl(|v_{x}|_{\infty}+|\eta_{t}|_{\infty}\bigr)|v_{t}|_{2}^{2}+|g|_{2}|v_{t}|_{2}.$ Thanks to the expressions ( 4.68 ) and ( 4.70 ), using the assumptions ( 4.74 ) and ( 4.75 ), we estimate the source terms $|f|_{2}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|f_{x}|_{2}$ and $|g|_{2}$ as follows (4.87) $\displaystyle|f|_{2}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|f_{x}|_{2}+|g|_{2}\lesssim\epsilon\bigl(|\eta_{x}|_{\infty}+|v|_{\infty}+|v_{x}|_{\infty}+|\eta_{t}|_{\infty}\bigr)$ $\displaystyle\times\bigl(|\eta_{x}|_{2}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\eta_{xx}|_{2}+\epsilon|\eta_{xxx}|_{2}+|v_{t}|_{2}+|v_{x}|_{2}+\epsilon|v_{xx}|_{2}\bigr).$ Now, we define $E_{1}(t)\underset{\text{def}}{=}E_{11}(t)+E_{12}(t)$ . Then ( 4.80 ) and ( 4.84 ) yields (4.88) $\displaystyle E_{1}(t)$ $\displaystyle\sim|\eta_{x}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|\eta_{t}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|v_{x}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|v_{t}|_{2}^{2}.$ where $|\cdot|_{X^{s}_{\epsilon^{k}}}^{2}=|\cdot|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\epsilon^{k}|\cdot|_{H^{s+k}}^{2}$ . Combining estimates ( 4.82 ), ( 4.86 ) and ( 4.87 ), using ( 4.88 ), we obtain (4.89) $\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}$ $\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E_{1}(t)\lesssim\epsilon\bigl(|\eta_{t}|_{\infty}+|\eta_{x}|_{\infty}+|v|_{\infty}+|v_{x}|_{\infty}\bigr)E_{1}(t),\quad t\in[0,T].$ Step 3.3. Estimates for ( 4.72 ). Since ( 4.72 ) has the same form as ( 4.71 ), we have a similar estimate as ( 4.86 ) for the second equation of ( 4.72 ), that is, (4.90) $\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{22}(t)\lesssim\epsilon\bigl(|v_{x}|_{\infty}+|\eta_{t}|_{\infty}\bigr)|v_{t}^{\prime}|_{2}^{2}+|g^{\prime}|_{2}|v_{t}^{\prime}|_{2},$ where (4.91) $\displaystyle E_{22}(t)$ $\displaystyle\underset{\text{def}}{=}(\frac{v_{t}^{\prime}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\,|\,v_{t}^{\prime})_{2}+|v_{x}^{\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|v_{xx}^{\prime}|_{2}^{2}$ $\displaystyle\sim|v_{t}^{\prime}|_{2}^{2}+|v_{x}^{\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|v_{xx}^{\prime}|_{2}^{2}\sim|v_{t}^{\prime}|_{2}^{2}+|v^{\prime}_{x}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}^{2}.$ Taking the $L^{2}$ scalar product of the first equation of ( 4.72 ) with $(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t}^{\prime}$ , we obtain (see the similar derivation of ( 4.79 )) that (4.92) $\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{21}(t)+(\frac{2\epsilon v}{1+\epsilon\eta}\partial_{x}\eta_{t}^{\prime}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t}^{\prime})_{2}$ $\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\eta_{t}\eta_{x}^{\prime}\,|\,\eta_{x}^{\prime})_{2}+\epsilon^{2}(\eta_{t}\eta_{xx}^{\prime}\,|\,\eta_{xx}^{\prime})_{2}+\epsilon^{2}(\eta_{xx}\eta_{x}^{\prime})\,|\,\eta_{tx}^{\prime})_{2}$ $\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon^{3}}{2}(\eta_{t}\eta_{xxx}^{\prime}\,|\,\eta_{xxx}^{\prime})_{2}+(f^{\prime}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{t}^{\prime})_{2}$ where $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} E_{21}(t)&\underset{\text{def}}{=}|\eta_{t}^{\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\eta_{tx}^{\prime}|_{2}^{2}+((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{x}^{\prime}\,|\,\eta_{x}^{\prime})_{2}+2\epsilon((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{xx}^{\prime}\,|\,\eta_{xx}^{\prime})_{2}\\
&\quad+\epsilon^{2}((1+\epsilon\eta)\eta_{xxx}^{\prime}\,|\,\eta_{xxx}^{\prime})_{2}.\end{aligned}$ Thanks to ( 4.64 ) and ( 4.75 ), we have (4.93) $E_{21}(t)\sim|\eta_{t}^{\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\eta_{tx}^{\prime}|_{2}^{2}+|\eta_{x}^{\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\eta_{xx}^{\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\eta_{xxx}^{\prime}|_{2}^{2}\sim|\eta_{t}^{\prime}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|\eta^{\prime}_{x}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}.$ Similarly to the derivation of ( 4.82 ), we obtain that (4.94) $\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{21}(t)\lesssim$ $\displaystyle\epsilon\bigl(|\eta_{t}|_{\infty}+|\eta_{x}|_{\infty}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\eta_{xx}|_{\infty}+|v_{x}|_{\infty}\bigr)E_{21}(t)$ $\displaystyle+|f^{\prime}|_{2}|\eta_{t}^{\prime}|_{2}+\epsilon|f_{x}^{\prime}|_{2}|\eta_{tx}^{\prime}|_{2}.$ In order to get the final estimate on system ( 4.72 ), we have to estimate the source terms $|f^{\prime}|_{2}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|f_{x}^{\prime}|_{2}$ and $|g^{\prime}|_{2}$ . Thanks to the expressions of $f^{\prime}$ and $g^{\prime}$ in ( 4.73 ) and the expressions of $f$ and $g$ in ( 4.68 ) and ( 4.70 ), using ( 4.74 ) and ( 4.75 ), after tedious but elementary calculations, we obtain that (4.95) $\displaystyle|f^{\prime}|_{2}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|f_{x}^{\prime}|_{2}+|g^{\prime}|_{2}\lesssim\epsilon\bigl(|\eta_{x}|_{\infty}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\eta_{xx}|_{\infty}+\epsilon|\eta_{xxx}|_{\infty}+|\eta_{t}|_{\infty}$ $\displaystyle+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\eta_{tx}|_{\infty}+|v_{x}|_{\infty}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|v_{xx}|_{\infty}+|v_{t}|_{\infty}\bigr)\bigl(|\eta_{xx}|_{2}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\eta_{xxx}|_{2}$ $\displaystyle+\epsilon|\eta_{xxxx}|_{2}+\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}|\eta_{xxxxx}|_{2}+|\eta_{t}|_{2}+|\eta_{tx}|_{2}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\eta_{txx}|_{2}$ $\displaystyle+\epsilon|\eta_{txxx}|_{2}+|v_{xx}|_{2}+|v_{t}|_{2}+|v_{tx}|_{2}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|v_{txx}|_{2}+|v_{tt}|_{2}\bigr),$ where we used $\eta^{\prime}=\eta_{t}$ and $v^{\prime}=v_{t}$ . Now, we define $E_{2}(t)\underset{\text{def}}{=}E_{21}(t)+E_{22}(t)$ . Then ( 4.91 ) and ( 4.93 ) yields (4.96) $E_{2}(t)\sim|\eta_{t}^{\prime}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|\eta^{\prime}_{x}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|v_{t}^{\prime}|_{2}^{2}+|v^{\prime}_{x}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}^{2}.$ Thanks to ( 4.90 ), ( 4.94 ) and ( 4.95 ), using the interpolation inequality ( 4.2 ), we obtain that (4.97) $\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{2}(t)\lesssim\epsilon\bigl(|\eta_{x}|_{\infty}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\eta_{xx}|_{\infty}+\epsilon|\eta_{xxx}|_{\infty}+|\eta_{t}|_{\infty}$ $\displaystyle+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\eta_{tx}|_{\infty}+|v_{x}|_{\infty}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|v_{xx}|_{\infty}+|v_{t}|_{\infty}\bigr)$ $\displaystyle\times\bigl(|\eta|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}+|\eta_{t}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|\eta_{tt}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|v|_{H^{2}}^{2}+|v_{t}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|v_{tt}|_{2}^{2}\bigr),$ where we replaced $\eta^{\prime},\ v^{\prime}$ by $\eta_{t},\ v_{t}$ respectively in the bound. Step 4. The final estimate on ( 4.66 ). Before closing the a priori estimates , we first define the energy functional associated to the quasilinear system ( 4.66 )-( 4.71 )-( 4.72 ) as (4.98) $E(t)\underset{\text{def}}{=}E_{0}(t)+E_{1}(t)+E_{2}(t).$ Notice that $\eta^{\prime}=\eta_{t}$ and $v^{\prime}=v_{t}$ . Then ( 4.77 ), ( 4.88 ) and ( 4.96 ) yield that (4.99) $\displaystyle E(t)\sim|\eta|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|\eta_{t}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|\eta_{tt}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|v|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|v_{t}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|v_{tt}|_{2}^{2}.$ In order to close the energy estimate, we also need to define the total energy functional for ( 4.66 ) as follows: (4.100) $\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)\underset{\text{def}}{=}|\eta|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}+|\eta_{t}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|\eta_{tt}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|v|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|v_{t}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|v_{tt}|_{2}^{2}.$ With the definitions ( 4.98 ) and ( 4.100 ), using the interpolation inequality ( 4.2 ) and the Sobolev inequality $|\cdot|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim|\cdot|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}$ , the energy estimates ( 4.78 ), ( 4.89 ) and ( 4.97 ) give rise to (4.101) $\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E(t)\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{E}(t)^{\frac{3}{2}}.$ To finish the proof, we have to show that (4.102) $\mathcal{E}(t)\sim E(t).$ Indeed, thanks to ( 4.99 ) and ( 4.100 ), we have $\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)\sim E(t)+|\eta|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}+|v|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}.$ Then we only need to show that $\displaystyle|\eta|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}+|v|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}\lesssim E(t).$ That is to say, we shall recover the regularity in space through the regularity in time.
More precisely, ( 4.66 ) yields (4.103) $v_{x}=-\eta_{t},\quad(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{x}=-\frac{v_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}-\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigl(\frac{v^{2}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{x}.$ The first equation of ( 4.103 ) shows (4.104) $|v|_{X_{\epsilon^{2}}^{2}}^{2}=|v|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|v|_{H^{2+2}}^{2}\lesssim|v|_{H^{1}}^{2}+|\eta_{tx}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\eta_{txxx}|_{2}^{2}\lesssim E(t),$ where we used ( 4.75 ).
While the second equation of ( 4.103 ), ( 4.75 ) and ( 4.104 ) imply $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &|\eta|_{X_{\epsilon^{3}}^{2}}^{2}\sim|\eta|_{H^{1}}^{2}+|(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{xx}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\eta_{xxx}|_{2}^{2}\\
&\lesssim|\eta|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|v|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|v_{t}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}^{2}\lesssim E(t),\end{aligned}$ which achieves the proof of ( 4.102 ). Due to ( 4.102 ) and ( 4.101 ), we have (4.105) $\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E(t)\lesssim\epsilon E(t)^{\frac{3}{2}}.$ Step 5. Initial data for the quasilinear system and final estimate. In this step, we have to derive the regularity of the initial data to the quasilinear system through the system ( 4.66 ) and the regularity of the initial data $(\eta_{0},v_{0})$ . The first equation of ( 4.66 ) shows that $\displaystyle|\eta^{\prime}|_{t=0}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}=|\eta_{t}|_{t=0}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}=|\partial_{x}v_{0}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}\lesssim|v_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}},$ while the second equation of ( 4.66 ) shows that $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad|v^{\prime}|_{t=0}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}=|v_{t}|_{t=0}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}\\
&\lesssim|(1+\epsilon\eta_{0})(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\partial_{x}\eta_{0}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}+\epsilon|\Bigl(\frac{v_{0}^{2}}{1+\epsilon\eta_{0}}\Bigr)_{x}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}\\
&\lesssim|\eta_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}+|v_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}},\end{aligned}$ where we assume that $|\eta_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}+|v_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}\leq C$ and $\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{0}$ with $\epsilon_{0}$ small enough. Thanks to ( 4.72 ), we can also infer that $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &|\eta^{\prime}_{t}|_{t=0}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}+|v^{\prime}_{t}|_{t=0}|_{2}=|\eta_{tt}|_{t=0}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}+|v_{tt}|_{t=0}|_{2}\\
&\lesssim|\eta_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}+|v_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}\end{aligned}$ provided that $|\eta_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}+|v_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}\leq C$ and $\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{0}$ with $\epsilon_{0}$ small enough. Thus, we have (4.106) $E(0)\sim\mathcal{E}(0)\lesssim|\eta_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}+|v_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}.$ Step 6. Existence and uniqueness. The estimates ( 4.105 ) and ( 4.106 ) are crucial to prove the existence of $T>0$ independent of $\epsilon$ such that ( 4.66 ) has a unique solution $(\eta,v)$ on a time interval $[0,T/\epsilon]$ with initial data $(\eta_{0},v_{0})\in X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}\times X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}$ satisfying moreover by ( 4.105 ) and ( 4.102 ) the estimate (4.107) $\sup_{t\in[0,T/\epsilon]}\mathcal{E}(t)\lesssim|\eta_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}+|v_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}.$ We shall precise the existence proof in the following Section 5. Notice that $v=(1+\epsilon\eta)u$ . Then we have obtained the long time estimate of solutions to the original Boussinesq system ( 1.2 )-( 4.1 ) with $a=b=d=0,c=-1$ together to the energy estimate ( 4.65 ).
∎ Now we state the long time existence result for the two-dimensional case. Theorem 4.7 . Let $a=b=d=0,c=-1$ .
Assume that $\eta_{0}\in X^{3}_{\epsilon^{4}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in X^{3}_{\epsilon^{3}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})$ satisfy the curl free condition $\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{u}_{0}=0$ and the (non-cavitation) condition (4.108) $1+\epsilon\eta_{0}\geq H>0,\quad H\in(0,1).$ Then there exists a constant $\tilde{c}_{0}$ such that for any $\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{0}=\frac{1}{\tilde{c}_{0}(|\eta_{0}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{4}}}+|u_{0}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{3}}})}$ , there
exists $T>0$ independent of $\epsilon$ , such that ( 1.2 )-( 4.1 ) has a unique solution $(\eta,\boldsymbol{u})^{T}$ with $\eta\in C([0,T/\epsilon];X^{3}_{\epsilon^{4}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))$ and $\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T/\epsilon];X^{3}_{\epsilon^{3}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))$ . Moreover, (4.109) $\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T/\epsilon]}\bigl(|\eta|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{4}}}^{2}+|\eta_{t}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}+|\eta_{tt}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|\eta_{ttt}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{u}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}$ $\displaystyle+|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{u}_{ttt}|_{2}^{2}\bigr)\leq C\bigl(|\eta_{0}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{4}}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}\bigr).$ Remark 4.5 . By simplicity, we assume that $\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{u}_{0}=0$ . Actually, the equation of $\boldsymbol{u}$ shows that $\partial_{t}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{u}(t,\cdot)=0$ so that $\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{u}$ is preserved as time evolves. In fact, as pointed out to us by Vincent Duchêne, considering the term $\nabla(|{\bf u}|^{2})$ is not physically relevant outside the irrotational case. When $\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits{\bf u}\neq 0,$ one should use instead the term ${\bf u}\cdot\nabla{\bf u},$ but then the corresponding system is to our knowledge not rigorously justified (see [ 12 ] for Green-Naghdi type systems). Proof. Since the proof is similar to that of Theorem 4.6 , we only sketch it. We also divide the proof into several steps. Again we only indicate how to obtain the a priori estimates. The existence proof which is similar to the one-dimensional case is postponed to the following Section 5. Step 1. Reduction of the system. Since $a=b=d=0,c=-1$ , we first rewrite ( 1.2 ) in the form ( 3.26 ). Setting $\displaystyle\boldsymbol{v}\underset{\text{def}}{=}(1+\epsilon\eta)\boldsymbol{u},$ the first equation of ( 3.26 ) becomes $\displaystyle\eta_{t}+\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}=0.$ To get the evolution equation for $\boldsymbol{v}$ , we first get from the second equation of ( 3.26 ) that $\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{u}=0,$ which along with the assumption that $\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{u}_{0}=0$ implies that $\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{u}=0$ . Then $\nabla(|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2})=2\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}$ and the second equation of ( 3.26 ) becomes to $\displaystyle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}+\nabla\eta-\epsilon\nabla\Delta\eta+\epsilon\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{0}.$ Similarly as one-dimensional case, elementary calculations and the use of the above equation yield the evolution equation for $\boldsymbol{v}:$ $\displaystyle\boldsymbol{v}_{t}+(1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\eta-\epsilon(1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\Delta\eta+\epsilon\nabla\cdot\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\otimes\boldsymbol{v}\Bigr)=\boldsymbol{0},$ where $\Bigl(\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{u}\otimes\boldsymbol{v})\Bigr)^{i}\underset{\text{def}}{=}\partial_{j}(u^{i}v^{j})$ .
Then ( 3.26 ) is rewritten in terms of $(\eta,\boldsymbol{v})$ as follows (4.110) $\left\{\begin{aligned} &\eta_{t}+\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}=0,\\
&\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}\boldsymbol{v}_{t}+\nabla\eta-\epsilon\nabla\Delta\eta+\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\eta}\nabla\cdot\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\otimes\boldsymbol{v}\Bigr)=\boldsymbol{0}.\end{aligned}\right.$ We shall derive energy estimates for this system. Step 2. Quasilinearization of ( 4.110 ). In this step, we shall quasilinearize the system ( 4.110 )
by applying to it $\partial_{t}$ , $\partial_{t}^{2}$ and $\partial_{t}^{3}$ . Applying $\partial_{t}$ to the first equation of ( 4.110 ) leads to $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}^{2}\eta&=-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t}=\nabla\cdot\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\eta\bigr)-\epsilon\nabla\cdot\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\Delta\eta\bigr)+\epsilon\nabla\cdot\Bigl[\nabla\cdot\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\otimes\boldsymbol{v}\Bigr)\Bigr]\\
&=\nabla\cdot\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\eta\bigr)-\epsilon\nabla\cdot\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\Delta\eta\bigr)+\frac{2\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v})+\frac{\epsilon|\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\\
&\qquad+2\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}\cdot\nabla\bigl(\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v})+\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}\cdot\nabla\bigl[\boldsymbol{v}\cdot\nabla\bigl(\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)\bigr]+\sum_{i,j=1,2}\partial_{j}\bigl(\frac{v^{i}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)\partial_{i}v^{j}.\end{aligned}$ One notices that the third term $\frac{2\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v})$ in the second line of the above equality is the higher order term. Since by ( 4.110 ) $\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}=-\eta_{t}$ , we rewrite this term as $\displaystyle\frac{2\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v})=-\frac{2\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla\eta_{t}.$ Then we obtain (4.111) $\eta_{tt}-\nabla\cdot\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\eta\bigr)+\epsilon\nabla\cdot\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\Delta\eta\bigr)+\frac{2\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla\eta_{t}=f,$ with (4.112) $\displaystyle f\underset{\text{def}}{=}$ $\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon|\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}}{1+\epsilon\eta}+2\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}\cdot\nabla\bigl(\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v})$ $\displaystyle+\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}\cdot\nabla\bigl[\boldsymbol{v}\cdot\nabla\bigl(\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)\bigr]+\epsilon\sum_{i,j=1,2}\partial_{j}\bigl(\frac{v^{i}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)\partial_{i}v^{j}.$ Applying $\partial_{t}$ to the second equation of ( 4.110 ) one obtains $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{v}&=-(1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\eta_{t}+\epsilon(1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\Delta\eta_{t}-\epsilon\eta_{t}(\nabla\eta-\epsilon\nabla\Delta\eta)-\epsilon\nabla\cdot\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\otimes\boldsymbol{v}\Bigr)_{t}.\end{aligned}$ Notice that $\eta_{t}=-\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}$ ,
we obtain the reduced equation for $\boldsymbol{v}$ (4.113) $\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}\boldsymbol{v}_{tt}-\nabla(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v})+\epsilon\nabla\Delta(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v})+(\frac{\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla)\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)$ $\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t})=\boldsymbol{g},$ with (4.114) $\displaystyle\boldsymbol{g}\underset{\text{def}}{=}$ $\displaystyle-\frac{\epsilon\eta_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}(\nabla\eta-\epsilon\nabla\Delta\eta)+(\frac{\epsilon^{2}\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla)\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}\eta_{t}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\Bigr)$ $\displaystyle-\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\eta}\partial_{t}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}).$ Combining ( 4.111 ) and ( 4.113 ), we obtain (4.115) $\left\{\begin{aligned} &\eta_{tt}-\nabla\cdot\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\eta\bigr)+\epsilon\nabla\cdot\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\Delta\eta\bigr)+\frac{2\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla\eta_{t}=f,\\
&\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}\boldsymbol{v}_{tt}-\nabla(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v})+\epsilon\nabla\Delta(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v})\\
&\qquad+(\frac{\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla)\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)+\frac{\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t})=\boldsymbol{g},\end{aligned}\right.$ with $(f,\boldsymbol{g})$ being defined in ( 4.112 ) and ( 4.114 ). We also remark here that ( 4.115 ) is a diagonalization of ( 4.110 ) and that the principal linear part for both equations of ( 4.115 ) is the dispersive wave $\displaystyle(\partial_{t}^{2}-\Delta+\epsilon\Delta^{2})\Psi.$ The source terms $(f,\boldsymbol{g})$ are of lower order. One can then derive the $L^{2}$ energy estimate for ( 4.115 ). Denoting by $\eta^{\prime}=\partial_{t}\eta$ and $\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{v}$ , applying $\partial_{t}$ to ( 4.115 ), it transpires that $(\eta^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime})$ satisfies the following system (4.116) $\left\{\begin{aligned} &\eta_{tt}^{\prime}-\nabla\cdot\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\eta^{\prime}\bigr)+\epsilon\nabla\cdot\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\Delta\eta^{\prime}\bigr)+\frac{2\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime}=f^{\prime},\\
&\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}\boldsymbol{v}_{tt}^{\prime}-\nabla(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime})+\epsilon\nabla\Delta(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime})\\
&\qquad+(\frac{\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla)\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)+\frac{\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime})=\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime},\end{aligned}\right.$ where (4.117) $\displaystyle f^{\prime}\underset{\text{def}}{=}\partial_{t}f+\epsilon\nabla\cdot(\eta_{t}\nabla\eta)-\epsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot(\eta_{t}\nabla\Delta\eta)-2\epsilon\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{t}\cdot\nabla\eta_{t}$ $\displaystyle\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}\underset{\text{def}}{=}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{g}+\frac{\epsilon\eta_{t}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}_{t}-\epsilon(\partial_{t}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)\cdot\nabla)\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)$ $\displaystyle+\epsilon^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla\Bigr)\Bigl(\frac{\eta_{t}\boldsymbol{v}_{t}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\Bigr)-\epsilon\partial_{t}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\Bigr)(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t}).$ The principal part of ( 4.116 ) is the same as that of ( 4.115 ). Similarly, denoting by $\eta^{\prime\prime}=\partial_{t}^{2}\eta=\partial_{t}\eta^{\prime}$ and $\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}=\partial_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{v}=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}$ , applying $\partial_{t}$ to ( 4.116 ), it transpires that $(\eta^{\prime\prime},\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime})$ satisfies the following system (4.118) $\left\{\begin{aligned} &\eta_{tt}^{\prime\prime}-\nabla\cdot\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\eta^{\prime\prime}\bigr)+\epsilon\nabla\cdot\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\Delta\eta^{\prime\prime}\bigr)+\frac{2\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}=f^{\prime\prime},\\
&\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}\boldsymbol{v}_{tt}^{\prime\prime}-\nabla(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime})+\epsilon\nabla\Delta(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime})\\
&\qquad+\epsilon(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla)\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)+\frac{\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime})=\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime\prime},\end{aligned}\right.$ where (4.119) $\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime}\underset{\text{def}}{=}\partial_{t}f^{\prime}+\epsilon\nabla\cdot(\eta_{t}\nabla\eta^{\prime})-\epsilon^{2}\nabla\cdot(\eta_{t}\nabla\Delta\eta^{\prime})-2\epsilon\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{t}\cdot\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime}$ $\displaystyle\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime\prime}\underset{\text{def}}{=}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}+\frac{\epsilon\eta_{t}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}_{tt}-\epsilon(\partial_{t}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)\cdot\nabla)\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)$ $\displaystyle+\epsilon^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla\Bigr)\Bigl(\frac{\eta_{t}\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}_{t}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\Bigr)-\epsilon\partial_{t}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\Bigr)(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime}).$ The principal part of ( 4.118 ) is also the same as that of ( 4.115 ). Step 3. Energy estimates for the quasilinear system ( 4.110 )-( 4.115 )-( 4.116 )-( 4.118 ). We shall derive energy estimates for ( 4.110 ), ( 4.115 ), ( 4.116 ), ( 4.118 ) under the assumptions (4.120) $1+\epsilon\eta\geq H>0,$ and for all $t\in[0,T]$ , (4.121) $|\eta(\cdot,t)|_{W^{1,\infty}}+|\boldsymbol{v}(\cdot,t)|_{W^{1,\infty}}+|\eta(\cdot,t)_{t}|_{\infty}+|\boldsymbol{v}(\cdot,t)_{t}|_{\infty}+|\eta(\cdot,t)|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{4}}}\leq c.$ where $|\cdot|_{X^{s}_{\epsilon^{k}}}^{2}=|\cdot|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\epsilon^{k}|\cdot|_{H^{s+k}}^{2}$ and the constant $c$ independent of $\epsilon$ depends on the initial data. We remark that ( 4.120 ) and ( 4.121 ) are consequence of the assumption ( 4.108 ) and the a priori estimate ( 4.150 ) for $(\eta,\boldsymbol{v})$ . Step 3.1. Estimates for ( 4.110 ). Similarly as ( 4.66 ), taking the $L^{2}$ inner product of ( 4.110 ) by $\bigl((1-\epsilon\Delta)\eta,\boldsymbol{v}\bigr)^{T}$ leads to (4.122) $\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{0}(t)=-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\bigl(\frac{\eta_{t}\boldsymbol{v}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\,|\,\boldsymbol{v}\bigr)_{2}-\epsilon\bigl(\nabla\cdot\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\otimes\boldsymbol{v}\Bigr)\,|\,\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)_{2},$ where $\displaystyle E_{0}(t)\underset{\text{def}}{=}|\eta|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\nabla\eta|_{2}^{2}+(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\,|\,\boldsymbol{v})_{2}.$ Thanks to ( 4.120 ) and ( 4.121 ), we have (4.123) $E_{0}(t)\sim|\eta|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\nabla\eta|_{2}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}|_{L^{2}}^{2}.$ By ( 4.120 ), the first term on the r.h.s of ( 4.122 ) is estimated as $\displaystyle|-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\bigl(\frac{\eta_{t}\boldsymbol{v}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\,|\,\boldsymbol{v}\bigr)_{2}|\lesssim\epsilon|\boldsymbol{v}|_{\infty}|\eta_{t}|_{2}|\boldsymbol{v}|_{2}\lesssim\epsilon|\boldsymbol{v}|_{\infty}(|\eta_{t}|_{2}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}|_{2}^{2}),$ while by integration by parts and ( 4.120 ), the second term on the r.h.s of ( 4.122 ) is estimated as $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &|-\epsilon\bigl(\nabla\cdot\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\otimes\boldsymbol{v}\Bigr)\,|\,\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)_{2}|=\frac{\epsilon}{2}|\bigl(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}\,|\,|\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}|^{2}\bigr)_{2}|\\
&\lesssim\epsilon|\boldsymbol{v}|_{\infty}|\boldsymbol{v}|_{2}|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}|_{2}\lesssim\epsilon|\boldsymbol{v}|_{\infty}(|\boldsymbol{v}|_{2}^{2}+|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}|_{2}^{2}).\end{aligned}$ Then we obtain (4.124) $\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{0}(t)\lesssim\epsilon|\boldsymbol{v}|_{\infty}\bigl(|\eta_{t}|_{2}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}|_{2}^{2}+|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}|_{2}^{2}\bigr).$ Step 3.2. Estimates for ( 4.118 ). Taking the $L^{2}$ scalar product of the first equation of ( 4.118 ) with $(1-\epsilon\Delta)\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}$ , we obtain $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &(\eta_{tt}^{\prime\prime}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\Delta)\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2}-(\nabla\cdot\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\eta^{\prime\prime}\bigr)\,|\,(1-\epsilon\Delta)\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2}\\
&\quad+\epsilon(\nabla\cdot\bigl((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\Delta\eta^{\prime\prime}\bigr)\,|\,(1-\epsilon\Delta)\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2}+(\frac{2\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\Delta)\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2}\\
&=(f^{\prime\prime}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\Delta)\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2}.\end{aligned}$ Similar to the derivation of ( 4.79 ), using integration by parts, we obtain (4.125) $\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{31}(t)+(\frac{2\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\Delta)\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2}$ $\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\eta_{t}\nabla\eta^{\prime\prime}\,|\,\nabla\eta^{\prime\prime})_{2}+\epsilon^{2}(\eta_{t}\Delta\eta^{\prime\prime}\,|\,\Delta\eta^{\prime\prime})_{2}+\epsilon^{2}(\nabla(\nabla\eta\cdot\nabla\eta^{\prime\prime})\,|\,\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2}$ $\displaystyle-\epsilon^{2}(\nabla\eta\Delta\eta^{\prime\prime}\,|\,\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2}+\frac{\epsilon^{3}}{2}(\eta_{t}\nabla\Delta\eta^{\prime\prime}\,|\,\nabla\Delta\eta^{\prime\prime})_{2}+(f^{\prime\prime}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\Delta)\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2}$ where $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} E_{31}(t)&\underset{\text{def}}{=}|\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\eta^{\prime\prime}\,|\,\nabla\eta^{\prime\prime})_{2}+2\epsilon((1+\epsilon\eta)\Delta\eta^{\prime\prime}\,|\,\Delta\eta^{\prime\prime})_{2}\\
&\quad+\epsilon^{2}((1+\epsilon\eta)\nabla\Delta\eta^{\prime\prime}\,|\,\nabla\Delta\eta^{\prime\prime})_{2}.\end{aligned}$ By ( 4.120 ) and ( 4.121 ), we have (4.126) $E_{31}(t)\sim|\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+|\nabla\eta^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\nabla^{2}\eta^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\nabla^{3}\eta^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}.$ Now, we estimate the second term on the l.h.s of ( 4.125 ). Integrating by parts, we have (4.127) $\displaystyle(\frac{2\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\cdot\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\,|\,(1-\epsilon\Delta)\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2}=-\epsilon(\nabla\cdot\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\,|\,\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2}$ $\displaystyle+2\epsilon^{2}(\bigl(\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\cdot\nabla\bigr)\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)\,|\,\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2}-\epsilon^{2}(\nabla\cdot\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\,|\,\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2},$ which along with ( 4.120 ), ( 4.121 )
and ( 4.125 ) implies that (4.128) $\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{31}(t)\lesssim\epsilon\bigl(|\eta_{t}|_{\infty}+|\nabla\eta|_{\infty}+|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}|_{\infty}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla^{2}\eta|_{\infty}\bigr)\bigl(|\nabla\eta^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\nabla^{2}\eta^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}$ $\displaystyle+\epsilon^{2}|\nabla^{3}\eta^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+|\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}\bigr)+|f^{\prime\prime}|_{2}|\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}+\epsilon|\nabla f^{\prime\prime}|_{2}|\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}.$ Taking the $L^{2}$ inner product of the second of ( 4.118 ) with $\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}$ yields (4.129) $\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{32}(t)+\epsilon\bigl((\boldsymbol{v}\cdot\nabla)\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)\,|\,\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)_{2}+\bigl(\frac{\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime})\,|\,\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}\bigr)_{2}$ $\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{t}\bigl(\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}\,|\,\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2}+(\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime\prime}\,|\,\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2},$ where $\displaystyle E_{32}(t)\underset{\text{def}}{=}(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\,|\,\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{2}+|\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\nabla(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime})|_{2}^{2}.$ Since $\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}}{1+\epsilon\eta})=0$ , we have $\displaystyle\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}=(1+\epsilon\eta)\bigl({v^{\prime\prime}}^{1}\partial_{2}(\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta})-{v^{\prime\prime}}^{2}\partial_{1}(\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\eta})\bigr)=\frac{\epsilon({v^{\prime\prime}}^{2}\partial_{1}\eta-{v^{\prime\prime}}^{1}\partial_{2}\eta)}{1+\epsilon\eta},$ which along with ( 4.120 ) and ( 4.121 ) shows that (4.130) $\displaystyle|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}\lesssim\epsilon|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2},$ $\displaystyle|\nabla(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime})|_{2}\lesssim\epsilon(|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}+|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2})|\eta|_{H^{3}}\lesssim\epsilon(|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}+|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}),$ where for the second inequality, we used the fact that $|\nabla\eta|_{\infty}\lesssim|\nabla\eta|_{H^{2}}$ and the following estimate $\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}\nabla^{2}\eta|_{2}\lesssim|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{4}|\nabla^{2}\eta|_{4}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Sobolev}}}{{\lesssim}}|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}|\nabla^{2}\eta|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{interpolation}}}{{\lesssim}}(|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}+|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2})|\nabla^{2}\eta|_{H^{1}}.$ Then by virtue of div-curl lemma, we obtain (4.131) $\displaystyle E_{32}(t)\sim|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+O(\epsilon|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}).$ Similar to ( 4.127 ), integration by parts on the second term on the l.h.s of ( 4.129 ) leads to (4.132) $\epsilon\bigl((\boldsymbol{v}\cdot\nabla)\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)\,|\,\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)_{2}=-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\bigl(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\,|\,\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)_{2}.$ For the third term on the l.h.s of ( 4.129 ), by integration by parts, we have $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\bigl(\frac{\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime})\,|\,\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}\bigr)_{2}=\epsilon\sum_{i,j=1,2}\bigl(\frac{v^{i}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}(\partial_{j}{v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{j})\,|\,{v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{i}\bigr)_{2}\\
&=-\epsilon\sum_{i,j=1,2}\bigl(\partial_{j}\bigl(\frac{v^{i}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\bigr){v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{j}\,|\,{v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{i}\bigr)_{2}-\epsilon\sum_{i,j=1,2}\bigl(\frac{v^{i}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}{v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{j}\,|\,\partial_{j}{v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{i}\bigr)_{2}.\end{aligned}$ To estimate the second term on the right hand side of the above equality, we first obtain by using $\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}}{1+\epsilon\eta})=0$ that $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{j}v^{\prime\prime i}_{t}&=\Bigl(\partial_{i}\bigl(\frac{v^{\prime\prime j}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)\cdot(1+\epsilon\eta)+\frac{\epsilon v^{\prime\prime i}\partial_{j}\eta}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{t}=\partial_{i}v^{\prime\prime j}_{t}+\epsilon\Bigl(\frac{v^{\prime\prime i}\partial_{j}\eta-v^{\prime\prime j}\partial_{i}\eta}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{t}.\end{aligned}$ By integration by parts, we have $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\ \ -\epsilon\bigl(\frac{v^{i}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}{v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{j}\,|\,\partial_{j}{v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{i}\bigr)_{2}\\
&=\frac{\epsilon}{2}\bigl(\partial_{i}\bigl(\frac{v^{i}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\bigr){v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{j}\,|\,{v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{j}\bigr)_{2}-\epsilon^{2}\bigl(\frac{v^{i}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}{v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{j}\,|\,\Bigl(\frac{v^{\prime\prime i}\partial_{j}\eta-v^{\prime\prime j}\partial_{i}\eta}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{t}\bigr)_{2}.\end{aligned}$ Then we obtain $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\bigl(\frac{\epsilon\boldsymbol{v}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime})\,|\,\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}\bigr)_{2}=\sum_{i,j=1,2}\Bigl(-\epsilon\bigl(\partial_{j}\bigl(\frac{v^{i}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\bigr){v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{j}\,|\,{v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{i}\bigr)_{2}\\
&\quad+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\bigl(\partial_{i}\bigl(\frac{v^{i}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}\bigr){v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{j}\,|\,{v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{j}\bigr)_{2}-\epsilon^{2}\bigl(\frac{v^{i}}{(1+\epsilon\eta)^{2}}{v_{t}^{\prime\prime}}^{j}\,|\,\Bigl(\frac{v^{\prime\prime i}\partial_{j}\eta-v^{\prime\prime j}\partial_{i}\eta}{1+\epsilon\eta}\Bigr)_{t}\bigr)_{2}\Bigr),\end{aligned}$ which along with ( 4.120 ), ( 4.121 ), ( 4.129 ) and ( 4.132 ) implies (4.133) $\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E_{32}(t)\lesssim$ $\displaystyle\epsilon\bigl(|\eta_{t}|_{\infty}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla\eta_{t}|_{4}+|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}|_{\infty}+|\nabla\eta|_{\infty}\bigr)$ $\displaystyle\times\bigl(|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}\bigr)+|\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}.$ Now, we define $E_{3}(t)\underset{\text{def}}{=}E_{31}(t)+E_{32}(t)$ . Then ( 4.126 ) and ( 4.131 ) yields (4.134) $\displaystyle E_{3}(t)$ $\displaystyle\sim|\nabla\eta^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\nabla^{2}\eta^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\nabla^{3}\eta^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+|\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}$ $\displaystyle+|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}+O(\epsilon|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}^{2}).$ Combining estimates ( 4.128 ) and ( 4.133 ), using ( 4.134 ), we obtain (4.135) $\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}$ $\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E_{3}(t)\lesssim\epsilon\bigl(|\eta_{t}|_{\infty}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla\eta_{t}|_{4}+|\nabla\eta|_{\infty}+|\nabla^{2}\eta|_{3}+|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}|_{\infty}\bigr)E_{3}(t)$ $\displaystyle+|f^{\prime\prime}|_{2}|\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}+\epsilon|\nabla f^{\prime\prime}|_{2}|\nabla\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}+|\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}.$ Step 3.3. Estimates for ( 4.115 ) and( 4.116 ). Since ( 4.115 ) and ( 4.116 ) have the same form as ( 4.118 ), we have similar estimates as ( 4.135 ) only with $(\eta^{\prime\prime},\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime})$ being replaced by $(\eta,\boldsymbol{v})$ and $(\eta^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime})$ respectively. In order to get the total estimate for system ( 4.115 ), ( 4.116 ) and ( 4.118 ), we have to estimate the source terms $|f|_{2}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla f|_{2}+|\boldsymbol{g}|_{2}$ , $|f^{\prime}|_{2}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla f^{\prime}|_{2}+|\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}|_{2}$ and $|f^{\prime\prime}|_{2}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla f^{\prime\prime}|_{2}+|\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}$ . Thanks to the expressions of $f$ , $\boldsymbol{g}$ , $f^{\prime}$ , $\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}$ and $f^{\prime\prime}$ , $\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime\prime}$ , using ( 4.120 ) and ( 4.121 ), after tedious but elementary calculations, we obtain (4.136) $\displaystyle|f|_{2}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla f|_{2}+|\boldsymbol{g}|_{2}+|f^{\prime}|_{2}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla f^{\prime}|_{2}+|\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}|_{2}$ $\displaystyle+|f^{\prime\prime}|_{2}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla f^{\prime\prime}|_{2}+|\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime\prime}|_{2}\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{E}(t),$ where (4.137) $\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)=$ $\displaystyle|\eta|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{4}}}^{2}+|\eta_{t}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}+|\eta_{tt}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|\eta_{ttt}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}$ $\displaystyle+|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}_{tt}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}_{ttt}|_{2}^{2}.$ In the process of derivation of ( 4.136 ), we used the fact that $\eta^{\prime}=\eta_{t},\,\eta^{\prime\prime}=\eta_{tt},\,\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{v}_{t},\,\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}=\boldsymbol{v}_{tt}$ and used the Hölder inequalities, Sobolev inequalities and interpolation inequalities frequently. We shall not show the details here. Step 4. The final estimate on ( 4.110 ). Before closing the a priori estimates , we first define the energy functional associated to the quasilinear system ( 4.110 )-( 4.115 )-( 4.116 )-( 4.118 ) as (4.138) $E(t)\underset{\text{def}}{=}E_{0}(t)+E_{1}(t)+E_{2}(t)+E_{3}(t),$ and $E_{1}(t),\,E_{2}(t)$ are defined in the same way as $E_{3}(t)$ with $(\eta^{\prime\prime},\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime})$ being replaced by $(\eta,\boldsymbol{v})$ and $(\eta^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime})$ respectively.
Notice that $\eta^{\prime}=\eta_{t},\,\eta^{\prime\prime}=\eta_{tt}$ and $\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{v}_{t},\,\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}=\boldsymbol{v}_{tt}$ . Then ( 4.123 ) and ( 4.134 ) yield (4.139) $\displaystyle E(t)\sim$ $\displaystyle|\eta|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|\eta_{t}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|\eta_{tt}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}+|\eta_{ttt}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}^{2}$ $\displaystyle+|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}_{tt}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}_{ttt}|_{2}^{2}.$ With the definitions ( 4.138 ) and ( 4.137 ), using the interpolation inequality ( 4.2 ) and the inequalities that $|u|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim|u|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}$ and $|u|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim|u|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla u|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\frac{1}{2}}$ , the energy estimates ( 4.124 ), ( 4.135 ) and ( 4.136 ) give rise to (4.140) $\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E(t)\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{E}(t)^{\frac{3}{2}},$ where $\mathcal{E}(t)$ is the total energy functional to ( 4.110 ) which is defined in ( 4.137 ). To finish the proof, we have to show that (4.141) $\mathcal{E}(t)\sim E(t).$ Indeed, thanks to ( 4.139 ) and ( 4.137 ), we have $\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)\sim E(t)+|\eta|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{4}}}^{2}+|\eta_{t}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}.$ Then we only need to show that $\displaystyle|\eta|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{4}}}^{2}+|\eta_{t}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}^{2}\lesssim E(t).$ That is to say, we shall recover the regularity in space through the regularity in time.
More precisely, ( 4.110 ) yields (4.142) $\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}=-\eta_{t},\quad(1-\epsilon\Delta)\nabla\eta=-\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}-\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\eta}\nabla\cdot\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\otimes\boldsymbol{v}\Bigr).$ To control $|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{X_{\epsilon^{2}}^{2}}$ , we first have $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{X_{\epsilon^{2}}^{2}}^{2}=|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{H^{2+2}}^{2}\\
&\lesssim|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{H^{1}}^{2}+|\nabla(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t})|_{2}^{2}+|\nabla(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{v}_{t})|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\nabla^{3}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t})|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\nabla^{3}(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{v}_{t})|_{2}^{2}.\end{aligned}$ Since $\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{t}}{1+\epsilon\eta}\bigr)=0$ , similar derivation as ( 4.130 ) leads to $\displaystyle\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{v}_{t}=\frac{\epsilon(v_{t}^{2}\partial_{1}\eta-v_{t}^{1}\partial_{2}\eta)}{1+\epsilon\eta},$ and $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &|\nabla(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{v}_{t})|_{2}\lesssim\epsilon(|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{2}+|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{2})|\eta|_{H^{3}}\lesssim\epsilon|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{H^{1}},\\
&|\nabla^{3}(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{v}_{t})|_{2}\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{2}+|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{2})|\eta|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon}}\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{H^{2}},\end{aligned}$ Then we have $\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{X_{\epsilon^{2}}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{H^{1}}^{2}+|\nabla(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t})|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\nabla^{3}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t})|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{3}|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{H^{2}}^{2},$ which along with the fact that $\epsilon$ is small enough implies $\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{X_{\epsilon^{2}}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{H^{1}}^{2}+|\nabla(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t})|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\nabla^{3}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{t})|_{2}^{2}.$ Now using the first equation of ( 4.142 ), we obtain that (4.143) $|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{X_{\epsilon^{2}}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{H^{1}}^{2}+|\nabla\eta_{tt}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\nabla^{3}\eta_{tt}|_{2}^{2}\lesssim E(t).$ Similarly, we obtain (4.144) $|\boldsymbol{v}|_{X_{\epsilon^{2}}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim|\boldsymbol{v}|_{H^{1}}^{2}+|\nabla\eta_{t}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\nabla^{3}\eta_{t}|_{2}^{2}\lesssim E(t).$ While the second equation of ( 4.142 ), ( 4.121 ), ( 4.143 ) and ( 4.144 ) imply (4.145) $\displaystyle|\eta_{t}|_{X_{\epsilon^{3}}^{2}}^{2}\sim|\eta_{t}|_{H^{1}}^{2}+|\nabla\bigl[(1-\epsilon\Delta)\nabla\eta_{t}\bigr]|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon|\nabla^{2}\bigl[(1-\epsilon\Delta)\nabla\eta_{t}\bigr]|_{2}^{2}$ $\displaystyle\lesssim|\eta_{t}|_{X_{\epsilon^{2}}^{1}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}_{tt}|_{X_{\epsilon}^{1}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{X_{\epsilon^{2}}^{2}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}|_{X_{\epsilon^{2}}^{2}}^{2}+|\eta|_{X_{\epsilon^{2}}^{1}}^{2}\lesssim E(t).$ To bound $|\boldsymbol{v}|_{X_{\epsilon^{3}}^{3}}$ , we first have $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad|\boldsymbol{v}|_{X_{\epsilon^{3}}^{3}}^{2}\sim|\boldsymbol{v}|_{H^{2}}^{2}+|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{v}|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{3}|\boldsymbol{v}|_{H^{3+3}}^{2}\\
&\lesssim|\boldsymbol{v}|_{H^{2}}^{2}+|\nabla^{2}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v})|_{2}^{2}+|\nabla^{2}(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{v})|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{3}|\nabla^{5}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v})|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{3}|\nabla^{5}(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{v})|_{2}^{2}.\end{aligned}$ Similar derivation as ( 4.130 ) yields $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &|\nabla^{2}(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{v})|_{2}\lesssim\epsilon|\boldsymbol{v}|_{H^{2}},\\
&\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla^{5}(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\boldsymbol{v}_{t})|_{2}^{2}\lesssim|\boldsymbol{v}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{2}}}|\eta|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{3}}}(1+|\eta|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon}})\lesssim|\boldsymbol{v}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{2}}}.\end{aligned}$ Then we obtain $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &|\boldsymbol{v}|_{X_{\epsilon^{3}}^{3}}^{2}\lesssim|\boldsymbol{v}|_{H^{2}}^{2}+|\nabla^{2}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v})|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{3}|\nabla^{5}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v})|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\boldsymbol{v}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{2}}},\end{aligned}$ which gives rise to $\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{v}|_{X_{\epsilon^{3}}^{3}}^{2}\lesssim|\boldsymbol{v}|_{H^{2}}^{2}+|\nabla^{2}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v})|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{3}|\nabla^{5}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v})|_{2}^{2}.$ Then using the first equation of ( 4.141 ), ( 4.145 ) and ( 4.139 ), we obtain (4.146) $|\boldsymbol{v}|_{X_{\epsilon^{3}}^{3}}^{2}\lesssim E(t).$ For $|\eta|_{X_{\epsilon^{4}}^{3}}^{2}$ , similar to the derivation of ( 4.145 ), by using the second equation of ( 4.141 ),
( 4.139 ), ( 4.143 ), ( 4.145 ) and ( 4.146 ), we finally obtain that (4.147) $\displaystyle|\eta|_{X_{\epsilon^{4}}^{3}}^{2}\sim|\eta|_{H^{1}}^{2}+|\nabla^{2}\bigl[(1-\epsilon\Delta)\nabla\eta\bigr]|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}|\nabla^{4}\bigl[(1-\epsilon\Delta)\nabla\eta\bigr]|_{2}^{2}\lesssim E(t).$ Due to ( 4.141 ) and ( 4.140 ), we have (4.148) $\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}E(t)\lesssim\epsilon E(t)^{\frac{3}{2}}.$ Step 5. Initial data for the quasilinear system and final estimate. In this step, we have to derive the regularity for the initial data to the quasilinear system through the system ( 4.110 ) and the regularity for initial data $(\eta_{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{0})$ . The first equation of ( 4.110 ) shows that $\displaystyle|\eta^{\prime}|_{t=0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}=|\eta_{t}|_{t=0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}=|\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{3}}}\lesssim|\boldsymbol{v}_{0}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{3}}},$ while the second equation of ( 4.110 ) shows that $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}|_{t=0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}=|\boldsymbol{v}_{t}|_{t=0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}\\
&\lesssim|(1+\epsilon\eta_{0})(1-\epsilon\Delta)\nabla\eta_{0}|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}+\epsilon|\nabla\cdot\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{0}}{1+\epsilon\eta_{0}}\otimes\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\Bigr)|_{X^{2}_{\epsilon^{2}}}\\
&\lesssim|\eta_{0}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{4}}}+|\boldsymbol{v}_{0}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{3}}},\end{aligned}$ where we assume that $|\eta_{0}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{4}}}+|\boldsymbol{v}_{0}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{3}}}\leq C$ and $\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{0}$ with $\epsilon_{0}$ small enough. Similarly, thanks to ( 4.116 ), we can obtain the upper bound of $|\eta^{\prime}_{t}|_{t=0}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}+|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}_{t}|_{t=0}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}$ ( or $|\eta_{tt}|_{t=0}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon^{2}}}+|\boldsymbol{v}_{tt}|_{t=0}|_{X^{1}_{\epsilon}}$ ). While by ( 4.118 ), we can also derive the upper bound for $|\eta^{\prime\prime}_{t}|_{t=0}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}+|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\prime}_{t}|_{t=0}|_{2}$ (or $|\eta_{ttt}|_{t=0}|_{X^{0}_{\epsilon}}+|\boldsymbol{v}_{ttt}|_{t=0}|_{2}$ ).
Then we finally obtain that (4.149) $E(0)\sim\mathcal{E}(0)\lesssim|\eta_{0}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{4}}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}_{0}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}.$ Step 6. Existence and uniqueness. The estimates ( 4.148 ) and ( 4.149 ) are crucial to prove the existence of $T>0$ independent of $\epsilon$ such that ( 4.110 ) has a unique solution $(\eta,\boldsymbol{v})$ on a time interval $[0,T/\epsilon]$ with initial data $(\eta_{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{0})\in X^{3}_{\epsilon^{4}}\times X^{3}_{\epsilon^{3}}$ satisfying moreover, by ( 4.148 ) and ( 4.141 ) the estimate (4.150) $\sup_{t\in[0,T/\epsilon]}\mathcal{E}(t)\lesssim|\eta_{0}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{4}}}^{2}+|\boldsymbol{v}_{0}|_{X^{3}_{\epsilon^{3}}}^{2}.$ The proof of the existence and uniqueness is postponed to Section 5. Notice that $\boldsymbol{v}=(1+\epsilon\eta)\boldsymbol{u}$ . Then we have obtained the long time estimate of solutions to the original Boussinesq system ( 1.2 )-( 4.1 ) with $a=b=d=0,c=-1$ together to the energy estimate ( 4.109 ).
∎ 5. Existence proof of Theorems 4.6 and 4.7 In this section, we shall complete the proof of existence and uniqueness of solutions to the transformed systems ( 4.66 ) and ( 4.110 ) so that we could complete the proofs to Theorems 4.6 and 4.7 . In order to construct the approximate solutions to ( 4.66 ) and ( 4.110 ), we introduce the mollifier operator $\mathcal{J}_{\delta}$ as follows (see [ 27 ] ): $\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{J}_{\delta}f}(\xi)=\varphi(\delta\xi)\hat{f}(\xi),\quad\forall\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\quad\forall f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),$ where $\varphi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})$ and $\varphi(0)=1$ . Then using Fourier transform, we obtain the following properties for $\mathcal{J}_{\delta}$ : Lemma 5.1 . For any $s,s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}$ and $1\leq p\leq\infty$ , there hold: (i) $|\mathcal{J}_{\delta}f|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}\leq C_{s,s^{\prime},\delta}|f|_{H^{s}}$ ; (ii) $|\mathcal{J}_{\delta}f|_{p}\leq C|f|_{p}$ ; (iii) $|\mathcal{J}_{\delta}f-f|_{H^{s-1}}\leq C\delta|f|_{H^{s}}$ ; (iv) $|\mathcal{J}_{\delta}f-f|_{H^{s}}\rightarrow 0$ as $\delta\rightarrow 0$ ; (v) $[\mathcal{J}_{\delta},a]f|_{H^{s}}\leq C|a|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|f|_{H^{s-1}}$ , for any $t_{0}\geq\frac{d}{2}$ and $-t_{0}<s\leq t_{0}+1$ ; where $C$ is an universal constant independent of $\delta$ and $C_{s,s^{\prime},\delta}$ is a constant depending on $s,s^{\prime},\delta$ . Proof. The statements (i), (iii) and (iv) are verified directly by Fourier analysis. For (ii), denoting by $\breve{\varphi}(\cdot)$ is the inverse Fourier transform of $\varphi$ . Then we have $\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\delta}f=\delta^{-d}\breve{\varphi}(\frac{\cdot}{d})*f.$ Notice that $\delta^{-d}|\breve{\varphi}(\frac{\cdot}{d})|_{1}\leq C$ . Then (ii) follows by Young inequality. The statement (v) is a consequence of Theorems 3 and 6 in [ 28 ] . Indeed, since $\mathcal{J}_{\delta}$ is a zeroth order Fourier multiplier, by [ 28 ] , we have $\displaystyle[\mathcal{J}_{\delta},a]f|_{H^{s}}\lesssim C(\varphi(\delta\cdot))|a|_{H^{t_{0}+1}}|f|_{H^{s}},$ where $\displaystyle C(\varphi(\delta\cdot))=\sup_{|\beta|\leq 2+d+[\frac{d}{2}]}\sup_{|\xi|\geq\frac{1}{4}}\langle\xi\rangle^{|\beta|}|\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\varphi(\delta\xi)|+\sup_{|\xi|\leq 1}|\varphi(\delta\xi)|\leq C.$ Thus, the lemma is proved.
∎ We only give the details of the existence proof to Theorem 4.6 . The existence proof of Theorem 4.7 follows a similar line. Now, we divide the proof into several steps. Step 1. Construction of the approximate solutions to ( 4.66 ). We construct an approximate solution sequence $\{(\eta^{\delta},\,v^{\delta})\}_{\delta>0}$ satisfying the following regularizing system (5.1) $\left\{\begin{aligned} &\eta_{t}^{\delta}+\mathcal{J}_{\delta}v^{\delta}_{x}=0\\
&v_{t}^{\delta}+(1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta})(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}_{x}+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{|\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}|^{2}}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}\Bigr)_{x}=0,\end{aligned}\right.$ associated with the initial data $\eta^{\delta}|_{t=0}=\eta_{0}$ and $v^{\delta}|_{t=0}=v_{0}$ . Denoting by $V^{\delta}=(\eta^{\delta},\ v^{\delta})$ , then ( 5.1 ) can be reduced to the following ODE in the Banach space $H^{m+1}\times H^{m}$ with $m\geq 0$ : (5.2) $\frac{d}{dt}V^{\delta}(t)=F_{\delta}(V^{\delta}),\quad V^{\delta}(0)=V^{\delta}_{0}\underset{\text{def}}{=}(\eta_{0},v_{0}),$ where $F_{\delta}(V^{\delta})=(F_{\delta}^{1}(V^{\delta}),F_{\delta}^{2}(V^{\delta}))$ with $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &F_{\delta}^{1}(V^{\delta})=-\mathcal{J}_{\delta}v^{\delta}_{x},\\
&F_{\delta}^{2}(V^{\delta})=-(1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta})(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}_{x}-\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{|\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}|^{2}}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}\Bigr)_{x}.\end{aligned}$ For any $V^{\delta}_{1}$ , $V^{\delta}_{2}$ , by virtue of the properties of $\mathcal{J}_{\delta}$ in Lemma 5.1 , we have $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |F_{\delta}^{1}(V^{\delta}_{1})-F_{\delta}^{1}(V^{\delta}_{2})|_{H^{m+1}}=|\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\partial_{x}(v^{\delta}_{1}-v^{\delta}_{2})|_{H^{m+1}}\leq C_{\delta,m}|v^{\delta}_{1}-v^{\delta}_{2}|_{H^{m}}.\end{aligned}$ Similarly, by Lemma 5.1 and the product estimate, we have $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &|F_{\delta}^{2}(V^{\delta}_{1})-F_{\delta}^{2}(V^{\delta}_{2})|_{H^{m}}\leq C_{\delta,m}(|\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}_{1}|_{H^{m}},|\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}_{2}|_{H^{m}},|\mathcal{J}_{\delta}v^{\delta}_{1}|_{H^{m}},|\mathcal{J}_{\delta}v^{\delta}_{2}|_{H^{m}})\\
&\qquad\times\bigl(|\eta^{\delta}_{1}-\eta^{\delta}_{2}|_{H^{m}}+|v^{\delta}_{1}-v^{\delta}_{2}|_{H^{m}}\bigr)\\
&\leq C_{\delta,m}(|V_{1}^{\delta}|_{2},|V_{2}^{\delta}|_{2})|V_{1}^{\delta}-V_{2}^{\delta}|_{H^{m+1}\times H^{m}},\end{aligned}$ where $C_{\delta,m}(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\cdots)$ is a constant depending on $\delta,m$ and $\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\cdots$ . Then we have $\displaystyle|F_{\delta}(V^{\delta}_{1})-F_{\delta}(V^{\delta}_{2})|_{H^{m+1}\times H^{m}}\leq C_{\delta,m}(|V_{1}^{\delta}|_{2},|V_{2}^{\delta}|_{2})|V_{1}^{\delta}-V_{2}^{\delta}|_{H^{m+1}\times H^{m}}$ so that $F_{\delta}(\cdot)$ is locally Lipschitz continuous on any open set $\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{M}=\{V\in H^{m+1}\times H^{m}(\mathbb{R})\,|\,|V|_{H^{m+1}\times H^{m}}\leq M\}.$ Thus, Picard (Cauchy-Lipschitz) existence theorem implies that, given any initial data $V_{0}\in H^{m+1}\times H^{m}(\mathbb{R})$ , there exists a unique solution $V^{\delta}\in C^{1}([0,T_{\delta});\mathcal{O}_{M}\cap(H^{m+1}\times H^{m}))$ for some $T_{\delta}>0$ , with any integer $m\geq 0$ . Going back to the regularizing system ( 5.1 ), since $V^{\delta}=(\eta^{\delta},v^{\delta})\in C^{1}([0,T_{\delta});H^{m+1}\times H^{m})$ , by virtue of the properties to $\mathcal{J}_{\delta}$ , we have $\displaystyle\partial_{t}V^{\delta}\in C^{1}([0,T_{\delta});H^{m+1}\times H^{m}),$ which implies $\displaystyle V^{\delta}\in C^{2}([0,T_{\delta});H^{m+1}\times H^{m}).$ Moreover, we could obtain that $\displaystyle V^{\delta}\in C^{k}([0,T_{\delta});H^{m+1}\times H^{m}),\quad\text{for any}\quad k\in\mathbb{N}.$ Thus, we could apply $\partial_{t}$ many times to ( 5.1 ). Step 2. Uniform energy estimates on the approximate solutions on some time interval $[0,T/\epsilon)$ . In this step, we shall prove that there exists a uniform existence time interval $[0,T/\epsilon)$ with $T$ being independent of $\delta$ and $\epsilon$ . To do so, we have to derive the uniform energy estimates for the approximate solutions $V^{\delta}$ . Step 2.1. The reduction equations. Motivated by the a priori energy estimates for ( 4.66 ) , we apply $\partial_{t}$ to ( 5.1 ). Similar derivation as ( 4.71 ), we obtain (5.3) $\left\{\begin{aligned} &\eta^{\delta}_{tt}-\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\partial_{x}\bigl((1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta})\partial_{x}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}\bigr)+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\partial_{x}\bigl((1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta})\partial_{x}^{3}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}\bigr)\\
&\quad+2\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}\cdot\partial_{x}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}\eta^{\delta}_{t}\Bigr)=f^{\delta},\\
&\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}v^{\delta}_{tt}-\partial_{x}^{2}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}+\epsilon\partial_{x}^{4}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}\\
&\quad+\frac{2\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}\Bigl(\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}\cdot\partial_{x}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}\bigl(\frac{v^{\delta}_{t}}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}\bigr)\Bigr)=g^{\delta},\end{aligned}\right.$ where $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} f^{\delta}&\underset{\text{def}}{=}\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{3}\Bigl[\Bigl(\frac{|\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}|^{2}}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}\Bigr)_{xx}-\frac{2\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}\cdot\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}_{xx}\Bigr]\\
&\quad-2\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}\bigl([\mathcal{J}_{\delta},\frac{\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}]\partial_{x}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}_{t}\bigr),\end{aligned}$ $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} g^{\delta}&\underset{\text{def}}{=}-\frac{\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}_{t}}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}(1-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2})\partial_{x}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}-\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}\partial_{x}\Bigl(|\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}|^{2}\bigl(\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}\bigr)_{t}\Bigr)\\
&\quad-\frac{2\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}_{x}}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}\cdot\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}_{t}-\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}\cdot\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}_{t}\bigl(\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}\bigr)_{x}\Bigr)\\
&\quad+\frac{2\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}\Bigl(\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}\cdot[\partial_{x}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2},\frac{1}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}]v^{\delta}_{t}\Bigr).\end{aligned}$ Similarly, applying $\partial_{t}$ to ( 5.3 ), denoting by $\eta^{{}^{\prime}\delta}=\partial_{t}\eta^{\delta},\,v^{{}^{\prime}\delta}=\partial_{t}v^{\delta}$ , we obtain (5.4) $\left\{\begin{aligned} &\eta^{\prime\delta}_{tt}-\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\partial_{x}\bigl((1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta})\partial_{x}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\prime\delta}\bigr)+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\partial_{x}\bigl((1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta})\partial_{x}^{3}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\prime\delta}\bigr)\\
&\quad+2\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}v^{\delta}}{1+\epsilon\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\eta^{\delta}}\cdot\partial_{x}\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{2}\eta^{\prime\delta}_{t}\Bigr)=f^{\prime\delta},\\
